What Australian Health Practitioner Tribunals Decide, and What Automated Analysis Misses: 3,586 Decisions, 1999 to February 2026

To fix (3)

To fix Marginal Significance

What we found

You described 1 effect with terms related to ‘marginally significant’.

Show details

You described effects with terms related to ‘marginally significant’. If p values above 0.05 are interpreted as an effect, you inflate the alpha level, and increase the Type 1 error rate. If a p value is higher than the prespecified alpha level, it should be interpreted as a non-significant result.

Text Section Header
Clinical competence failures fell from more than half of disciplinary decisions before 2015 to under a third from 2020, although the evidence is at the margin of statistical significance. Clinical Competence and Impairment

For metascientific articles demonstrating the rate at which non-significant p-values are interpreted as marginally significant, see:

Olsson-Collentine A, van Assen MALM, Hartgerink CHJ (2019). “The Prevalence of Marginally Significant Results in Psychology Over Time.” Psychological Science, 30, 576–586. doi:10.1177/0956797619830326.

For the list of terms used to identifify marginally significant results, see this blog post by Matthew Hankins.

To check (3)

To check Non-Significant P Value Check

What we found

We found 3 non-significant p values that should be checked for appropriate interpretation.

Show details

Meta-scientific research has shown nonsignificant p values are commonly misinterpreted. It is incorrect to infer that there is ‘no effect’, ‘no difference’, or that groups are ‘the same’ after p > 0.05.

It is possible that there is a true non-zero effect, but that the study did not detect it. Make sure your inference acknowledges that it is possible that there is a non-zero effect. It is correct to include the effect is ‘not significantly’ different, although this just restates that p > 0.05.

Metacheck does not yet analyze automatically whether sentences which include non-significant p-values are correct, but we recommend manually checking the sentences below for possible misinterpreted non-significant p values.

Text Sentence
p = 0.051 Clinical competence failures fell from 57.1% to 31.2% (odds ratio 0.56 per decade, 0.32-1.00, p = 0.051).
p = 0.329 Boundary violations were labelled in 74.4% of psychologist decisions, consistent with Kremer’s finding for psychologists. 10 The most severe outcome varied by jurisdiction (chi-square = 545.01, df = 56, FDR-adjusted p < 0.001, V = 0.177), most strongly between Queensland and both New South Wales (V = 0.491) and Victoria (V = 0.440); six of seven misconduct types also varied by jurisdiction, but boundary violations did not (V = 0.058, FDR-adjusted p = 0.329) (Supplementary Tables S9 and S10). 7).
p = 0.164 In the exploratory corpus-wide analysis, decisions labelled as boundary violations were not significantly more likely to end in cancellation than other disciplinary decisions (42.0% vs 38.7%; FDR-adjusted p = 0.164; Supplementary Table S7), unlike Millbank’s finding that proved sexual misconduct attracted the highest rate of removal from practice of any head of misconduct; 47 but the boundary category here includes non-sexual breaches, the comparison decisions also often end in cancellation, and the outcome labels over-detect cancellation.

For metascientific articles demonstrating the rate of misinterpretations of non-significant results is high, see:

Aczel B, Palfi B, Szollosi A, Kovacs M, Szaszi B, Szecsi P, Zrubka M, Gronau Q, van den Bergh D, Wagenmakers E (2018). “Quantifying Support for the Null Hypothesis in Psychology: An Empirical Investigation.” Advances in Methods and Practices in Psychological Science, 1(3), 357–366. doi:10.1177/2515245918773742.

Murphy S, Merz R, Reimann L, Fernández A (2025). “Nonsignificance misinterpreted as an effect’s absence in psychology: Prevalence and temporal analyses.” Royal Society Open Science, 12(3), 242167. doi:10.1098/rsos.242167.

For educational material on preventing the misinterpretation of p values, see Improving Your Statistical Inferences.

Absolute Paths

Best programming practice is to use relative file paths (e.g., ‘./files’) instead of absolute file paths (e.g., ‘C://Lakens/project_dir/files’) as these folder names do not exist on other computers. The following absolute file paths were found in 1 code file. However, these may be false positives in code like paste0(dir, '/file.csv').

File name Absolute paths found
legal_bert_training.ipynb /content/drive | /content/drive/MyDrive/health_misconduct_nlp

Working Directory (setwd)

Best programming practice is to avoid setwd() in analysis code: it hardcodes an assumption about the working directory (often an absolute path on the author’s own machine), so the code breaks when run anywhere else. Keep the working directory as the caller sets it and use relative paths. No setwd() calls were found in any of the code files.

Package Installation (install.packages)

Best programming practice is to avoid install.packages() in analysis code: it installs software on whoever runs the script without asking, which is at minimum disruptive (an unexpected install, network access, a package version the author never tested against) and at worst a security risk if it points at an untrusted repository. Document required packages instead (a README, or a list of them: an renv.lock/DESCRIPTION for R, a requirements.txt for Python). No install.packages() calls were found in any of the code files.

Libraries / Imports

Best programming practice is to load all required libraries/imports in one block near the top of the code. In 13 code files, libraries/imports were at multiple places (i.e., with more than 3 non-comment lines in between).

Careless Responding

No file could be screened for careless responding.

What this does not cover. Only 0 of 46 data files could be screened: the rest had no detectable multi-item scale block, or too few respondents. Even in a screened file, these checks only look at multi-item rating scales — the only place where a repeated or flat answer pattern is interpretable. Single questions, open text, demographics and any item outside a detected scale block are never examined. A respondent can therefore answer most of a survey carelessly and still not be flagged here, and finding nothing is not evidence that a dataset is free of careless responding.

How to make your code run on another computer

We ran your code on a clean computer, the way a reader or reviewer would. Below is each problem we found, the scripts it affects, and how to fix it. Fix them in the order given: a problem near the top can cause the ones below it.

Files are missing from your deposit.

  • score_llm_test2.py reads P13_test2_results_SUBMITTED_2026-11-07.xlsx.
  • build_gold_standard_2026.py reads P13_adjudication.xlsx.
  • irr_recode_2026.py reads P13_recode_results_SUBMITTED_2026-11-03.xlsx.
  • second_coder_agreement_2026.py reads P13_test2_results_SUBMITTED_2026-11-07.xlsx.
  • bertopic_analysis.py reads all_decisions.jsonl.
  • build_adjudication_sheet.py reads P13_recode_results_SUBMITTED_2026-11-03.xlsx.
  • build_supplementary_texts.py reads new_sample_300.jsonl.
  • check_adjudication.py reads P13_adjudication.xlsx.
  • check_recode.py reads P13_recode_results.xlsx.
  • data_preparation.py reads all_decisions.jsonl.
  • and 14 more

The script stops at the line that reads the file.

  1. Find the file on your computer.
  2. Add it to the deposit, in the folder where the script looks for it.
  3. If you cannot share it, for example for privacy reasons, say so in the README, and explain where readers can get it and where to put it.

A script starts a program outside R or Python.

  • build_poisons_schedule_map.py starts pandoc.

The computer we run the code on does not have this program, so we could not check these scripts; this need not be a fault of the code. Name the program, its version and where to get it in the README. A Docker image can include it.

A script runs only on one platform.

  • legal_bert_training.ipynb needs Google Colab.

We could not check these scripts, because the computer we run the code on is not that platform. Say in the README which platform the code needs. Where you can, let the analysis also run without it, for example by reading the data from files in the deposit.

A script waits for a person to choose a file, answer a question, or give arguments on the command line.

  • llm_annotation.py
  • model_second_coder.py

Nobody can answer when the code is checked automatically. Write the file name into the script, and put the exact command with its arguments in the README.

A script is part of a package of your code, and Python cannot run it on its own.

  • austlii_extractor.py
  • austlii_scraper.py
  • corpus_download_nonnsw.py
  • corpus_download.py
  • merge_corpus.py
  • run_pipeline.py
  • wayback_scraper.py

It imports other files of its package with from ., which works only when it is started as a module, as python -m package.module from the folder above the package. Put the command that starts it in the README.

Metacheck can’t automatically establish what caused the error, or how it should be improved.

  • irr_analysis.py
  • label_studio_import.py
  • llm_irr_comparison.py
  • registrant_rates.py

Execution

The Python code was run. Each script ran in an isolated Docker container against a throwaway copy of the repository, in the run order above, with a 300-second per-script timeout. Of 47 scripts: 3 ran without error, 40 errored, 0 timed out, 0 could not run because one of its own dependencies is unavailable, 0 did not parse, and 0 are written for Python 2 and not run. 2 files were not run on their own, because other files import them: config.py (by llm_irr_comparison.py, label_studio_import.py, llm_annotation.py, metadata_enrichment.py, sample_selection.py, austlii_extractor.py, austlii_scraper.py, corpus_download.py, corpus_download_nonnsw.py, filters.py, merge_corpus.py, wayback_scraper.py); filters.py (by austlii_extractor.py, austlii_scraper.py, corpus_download.py, corpus_download_nonnsw.py). Running against current package versions: a break can reflect version drift, which argues for pinning versions (e.g. in a requirements.txt). 3 scripts stopped on a file another script writes, so they were run once more after the other scripts: registrant_rates.py (enriched_corpus.jsonl); revision_analyses.py (enriched_corpus.jsonl); statistical_analysis.py (enriched_corpus.jsonl). Which script must run first can be stated in a README, or shown by numbering the file names (01_, 02_). A script wrote into a folder that the deposit does not have, so we made it and ran it once more: data/extracted for predict_full_corpus.py. Keeping the (empty) output folders in the deposit, or making them in the code with dir.create(), solves this.

Ethics Approval Check

Based on the following text, we would expect an ethics approval statement, and it was present:

The coder was recruited through the Prolific platform 29 from participants with a postgraduate degree in law or a health discipline, fluent English, and an approval rate of at least 99%.

The author thanks A.M., who independently coded the reliability subset as a paid coder recruited through Prolific.

It also staged the second coder’s Prolific studies and workbooks, froze the returned files, computed agreement from them, and drafted the descriptive classification of the coders’ disagreements by type (Supplementary Table S35), which the author reviewed; it did not create or change any coder’s codes.

Ethics approval statement:

No ethics approval was required.

Power Analysis Check

No power analyses were detected.

Power analyses need to contain the following information to be interpretable: the type of power analysis, the statistical test, the software used, sample size, critical alpha criterion, power level, effect size, and an effect size metric. In addition, it is recommended to make sure the power analysis is reproducible (by sharing the code, or a screenshot, of the power analysis), and to provide good arguments for why the study was designed to detect an effect of this size.

For an a-priori power analysis, where the sample size is determined, reporting all information would look like:

An a priori power analysis for an independent samples t-test, conducted using the pwr.t.test function from pwr (Champely, 2020), indicated that for a Cohen’s d = 0.5, an alpha level of 0.05, and a desired power level of 80% required at least 64 participants in each group.

For a sensitivity power analysis, this sentence would look like:

A sensitivity power analysis for an independent samples t-test, conducted using the pwr.t.test function from pwr (Champely, 2020), indicated that with 64 participants in each group, and an alpha level of 0.05, a desired power level of 80% was reached for an effect size of d = 0.5.
